ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u17	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u18	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law on debt restructuring of agricultural holding entities. The envisaged solutions shall be addressed to farmers operating at least 3 years before the date of the loss of the debt management capacity. However, entities in liquidation or bankruptcy and those in respect of which restructuring proceedings are pending under the provisions of the Law of 15 May 2015 will not be covered. The solutions under preparation include four options to support farmers who are insolvent or threatened with insolvency. These options include: aid for the interest rate on bank restructuring loans granted by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, loans to repay the debts of agricultural holding entities granted by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, guarantees for the repayment of bank restructuring loans granted by the National Agricultural Support Centre and the acquisition of the debt of agricultural holding entities by the National Agricultural Support Centre in exchange for the transfer of agricultural property ownership to the Treasury. The above is intended to allow the payment of old debt partly restructured in previous restructuring proceedings related solely to agricultural activities. The restructuring procedure may be initiated after the relevant entity has submitted a request. In the case of an application to the ARiMR, the head of the county office of the agency shall be responsible for the application to the KOWR, and the director of the field branch shall decide in the case of the application to the KOWR. It will be mandatory to add to each proposal a restructuring plan containing elements such as: a description of the measures planned to recover the financial liquidity of the holding, a timetable for the implementation of restructuring measures or an analysis and assessment of the economic situation of the farm operator. The restructuring shall be subject to debt of a monetary nature arising from the pursuit of agricultural activities, including capital, interest and other charges relating to debt management. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposal for a law on debt restructuring of farm operators, which responds to the demands of the agricultural community, and is in favour of directing it to further process. Thank you very much.

Therefore, the applicant should be accused of an exceptional lack of professionalism when trying to find solutions in the case of indebted farms. The government party for the purposes of this law stated that 1690 agricultural producers had a problem with the repayment of bank loans. As it turns out, the above data were collected between September and October 2017 and relate only to loans granted with State aid from the State's office through ARiMR. This pool shall not exceed 10.15% all loans granted, disregards any agricultural loans taken into account in cooperative banks and other forms of leasing and revolving loans for holdings not classified as strictly agricultural. The lack of verification of the scale of the debt problem of agricultural holdings disqualifies it as a form of genuine aid. According to our estimates, the debt problem of agricultural holdings is a problem of 20 and 30 thousand. farms with varying degrees of bankruptcy risk, with an increasing trend, particularly this year, in the year of severe drought. The proposed law is limited only to the above mentioned pool of approximately 1700 farms. In conclusion, once again, I would like to point out that the Government's proposal is a frivolous treatment of farmers and irresponsibleness by the authors of the project. I have a false hope of helping farmers from the state, and the truth is that only a handful of people in need will be able to count on any support. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u39	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u40	Thank you, Marshal. Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the draft Act on the Change of Energy Law (Print No 2911). The Commission took note of the draft law at its meeting on 24 October. In the course of the work, amendments adopted by the committee were proposed and the issue of further work on the project was welcomed. The aim of the project itself is among others.: regulation of the back-up selling institution, i.e. the sale of gas fuels or electricity to the final customer, connected to the electricity or gas distribution network or to the gas transmission network carried out by the back-up seller in the event that the sale of gas fuels or electricity by the former seller is discontinued. To this end, the project provides for changes resulting from the need to implement in the Polish legal order the network codes and guidelines adopted within the European Union, which constitute measures to build a common single market for electricity in the Community. The question of whether the device, installation or network is to be decided by the President of the URE is also subject to regulation. new or existing and general application requirements which include the conditions of connection to the network to be met by the device, installation or network. One of the most important changes is the introduction of a stock market obligation, i.e. the modification of the scope of the obligation to sell electricity produced by increasing its level from 30% to 100%, in order to improve the transparency of the energy market. There are currently situations where vertically integrated energy groups allow for trade in energy between entities within their structure, resulting in a significant part of the energy being avoided by the main stock market. Raising the obligation to 100% aims to reduce possible increases in electricity prices on the wholesale market that do not come from fundamental factors affecting the cost of generating electricity or the acquisition of neighbouring systems. At the committee meeting, amendments of an editorial and legislative nature were proposed, but also of a substantive nature. Their goal is among others.: extending the catalogue of entities informed by the seller to cease the performance of the contract also by a reserve seller so that he can be prepared as efficiently as possible to start the execution of the reserve sale, closing the catalogue of entities for which distribution and transmission system operators are required to provide information to each other, increasing the level of protection of customers of gas fuels and electricity before the supply of those media, extending the deadline for handing over a copy of the reserve contract or a comprehensive contract concluded on its behalf from 10 to 30 days due to doubts as to the possibility of simultaneously sending contracts to multiple customers, adapting the new provisions to the energy law system and increasing the flexibility of operators in determining the quantities consumed by the final customer of gas and electricity fuels, or maintaining contracts before the date of entry into force of the proposed regulation to all entities that have concluded an electricity sale agreement. Mrs. Marshal! Following a positive opinion from the committee, together with the amendments, we ask for further work on this project. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u41	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Beata Mateusiak-Pieluch will be the first to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u42	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the Government's draft law amending the Energy Law Act and certain other laws, since the project is compatible with European Union law and is not subject to the requirements for the presentation and receipt of opinions, consultations or agreements as regards the bodies and institutions of the European Union. The proposed amendments to the Energy Law comply with the obligation to implement network codes. Network codes and guidelines are part of building a common single energy market within the European Union. Since network codes are European Commission regulations, they are therefore in force for EU Member States. The proposed changes meet the requirements for us to build a single energy market. There are provisions in the current law relating to reserve sales, but they only apply to the sale of electricity. In the new wording, the Energy Law will define and regulate the issue of the sale of reserve electricity and gas fuels. The proposed amendment to the Act consists in completing the existing provisions with regard to reserve sales. The energy market must ensure continuity of electricity supply in order to function efficiently. This is due to the institution of the reserve energy seller - a company whose role is to care for customers who, due to the problems of their current energy seller, would theoretically be left without the seller. In market practice, the energy reserve seller is another active seller who operates on the same market as the relevant seller for a particular entity and has a signed contract with the distributor serving that entity. In a crisis situation, the backup seller is obliged to take over clients from the seller who made the contract. The proposed provisions in the Energy Law also define the obligations of the Energy Regulatory Office in the event of network codes. Since the transmission system operator only examines the requirements of formal notification and not whether the conditions laid down in the codes for the recognition of installations, installations or networks as existing are met, these obligations will be assumed by the President of the URE. In addition, the President of the URE will examine this and decide to consider the connected devices, installations or networks as existing or new. Network codes as a regulation of the Commission of the European Union due to their direct applicability since their entry into force form part of national law and are applied directly, without the need for transposition into national law. The establishment of any act of national law which would repeat the rules contained in the network codes would constitute an infringement of Union law, as it would limit the direct effectiveness of regulations as Union acts by withdrawing from the date of entry into force of the laws creating the rights of individuals. The proposed amendments to the Energy Law do not infringe and support the application of the regulations of the European Commission. It should also be noted that the introduction of an obligation to sell the energy generated in its entirety through the energy market exchange is beneficial. To date, this obligation only covered 30%. 100% sales through the listed energy market will improve transparency of trading and should effectively regulate possible price increases by taking advantage of the dominant position of individual energy market participants. This solution is beneficial to the public, and we share the Government's view that: "The above, in the opinion of the designer, will stabilise electricity prices on the wholesale market at a competitive level, thus preventing their unjustified growth for electricity consumers. This solution should improve the long-term position of consumers in the domestic electricity market. In favor of the bill, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I submit to the Marshal an amendment to the Act amending the Energy Law Act and some other laws in print No. 2911 and 2944 for further consideration. Thank you.

I would like to remind you that as the electricity exchange obligation was introduced at 30% or 55% in the case of gas, then on a good case some issues were left unregulated. In the case of electricity, the rules only applied to energy produced and kept silent about... establishing this as a basis for calculating percentages, so they did not include all production. Today, when all production is to be covered by the obligation, it is necessary to determine in some way what is going on with what has already been contracted. And here are two possibilities. The first possibility is once again to completely ignore the issue and hope that in each individual case disputes will be settled individually. It is possible to adopt a solution which boils down to the fact that the legislator by law will interfere with these agreements. Then we are dealing with a kind of expropriation from the rights deriving from these contracts, or we can respect those agreements and say that the basis for calculating the obligation does not cover what is covered by those contracts. In the submission, this is in the version of the committee's report, this is the solution that has been adopted. Thus, the legislator decided not to leave any unregulated area, I said here, this concerns all the energy generated, and decided to respect what has already been contracted, i.e. not to expropriate from the rights arising from the agreements concluded. [[That's the problem.]] If that's how we see it, because it fell out of the room, that's the problem, that's really the problem that's going to end over time. [[How long?]] It depends on individual contracting strategies. Nevertheless, this is something that is inevitable and will happen. It is a matter of fact that if someone, that I say so, decided to conclude contracts that he was fleeing from the obligation, it also means that he decided that he would accept other risks associated with this action, because he does not know the price of the fuel, he does not know the price of CO2, he must somehow secure it. It is not such a simple matter to contract for many years forward, because then it must accept in the dark risks, which are much worse than contracting at certain prices. Another thing that rained here is also a duty and is linked to prices, which is how the government intends to react in the situation that has been presented here, that it was actually more expensive in this one hour. Above all, yes: the obligation is to make the market more transparent. This is always a good solution if we know that all manufacturers are obliged to put their entire production on the market. This also improves the liquidity of this market, which also means that it is more difficult for this market to disrupt some incidents. Thirdly, if someone thinks that one of the players on the market can have the power to use it, that's how we take it away from him. Therefore, this is one of the key actions that the government has decided to take in order to protect consumers in the sense that a properly functioning market allows the right balance price to be established, whatever it is, but it would result from those behaviours that are on the market, only everyone must act so, honestly and in the same way. Complementarity is the provisions of this project governing reserve sales. Let me remind you that so far there has been a regulated back-up sale, concerning electricity. Now these provisions have been extended to the entire gas reserve sales department. We have previously noted such events that some vendors have started to have problems on the market, mainly due to the fact that they were leading a bad strategy or adopted the wrong contracting strategy, because if someone signs sales contracts without a secured supply side, they have a short position, then they are heavily exposed to various types of fluctuations in the market. Such a sales strategy, contracting the whole on the spot market, is wrong, by economic nature. [[It's okay.]] But we see that these things can happen, and so all the provisions on backup sales have been a little more developed in relation to what has been done and also supplemented with gas matters. As far as all questions about the price of energy are concerned, I assume that in the following years - we assume - after these regulations have been introduced, the transparency of the market and the improvement of the liquidity of the market the price will be the right price, the equilibrium price of this market. We are trying to eliminate all actions or all behaviours of market participants that could lead to a price that would deviate from the price of equilibrium. So this is about transparency, but also about the impact on the part of the price that results from manufacturing costs, for example CO2. Following the announcements of various types of action, this price fell to below EUR 20 today, and it is therefore clear that something has happened. Furthermore, we have also asked for the Commission to launch its actions. It is possible to convene a committee which may reflect on the various intervention measures in a situation where the price of emission allowances over a given period is more volatile than the average price over the last 2 years. As you can see, at this point, these actions have, at least in part, had an effect, because the price has begun to fall. According to the applicants, the amendments contained in the draft amendment to the Law of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of hard coal mining will be financed within the limits of the budgetary expenditure referred to in Article. 6 of the Law of 22 January 2015 amending the Law on the Functioning of Coal Mining in the years 2008 to 2015 and certain other laws. The extension of the funding period will not result in an increase in expenditure within the limit set out in the above provision. Therefore, I would very much like to ask the Minister to clarify a few doubts. Firstly, the European Commission's decision extends funding from the state budget for mine decommissioning to 2023 rather than restructuring costs for the mining sector. Using the explanatory statement: the costs of restructuring the mining sector, i.e. also active mines, is unauthorised, inaccurate and does not refer to the law on the operation of hard coal mining in the years 2008 to 2015. The aim is to sober up and disguise the actual intention of the law, which has been passed for a specific purpose, namely to finance the liquidation of the mine, the mining plant or its designated part. Secondly, the proposal to amend the Article is presented. Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 3 introduces a dangerous precedent in relation to the current wording, as it defines the mine decommissioning process as a process of decommissioning only bottom infrastructure, and all surface infrastructure activities are referred to as post-decommission operations. Such a provision is in principle an attempt to circumvent the deadlines for the closure of the mines indicated in the decommissioning programme. It allows to extend the decommissioning of mines, to postpone the deadline to the nearest indefinite date, which will therefore not be without an impact on the total cost of mine decommissioning. The liquidation of the mine shall mean the decommissioning of both the bottom and the surface infrastructure, including the sale of unnecessary assets, or the transfer of certain land or facilities, e.g. for the benefit of the municipalities. The only cost, which is difficult to determine, is the cost of mining damages, because they are difficult to estimate, because they can come out even up to a few years after the liquidation. This is, among others, the case. the cost of pumping water to prevent flooding and flooding of neighbouring mines. Therefore, the financial resources needed to eliminate mines should be separated from those necessary to cover the costs of mining damage and water pumping. I would ask that the Minister take this position into account in the further legislative process concerning this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u119	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 18 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law on the financial support for audiovisual production (prints No. 2794 and 2947). I ask Mrs Elżbieta Raven to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u120	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law on financial support for audiovisual production from print No. 2794. According to the justification of this draft law, the aim of the proposed submission of financial support for audiovisual production is the comprehensive development of the Polish audiovisual industry and the promotion of Polish cultural heritage in the world through financial support for the production of audiovisual works. The consequences of the introduction of new regulations are to improve the working conditions of the audiovisual industry in Poland and to create a modern system of financial support, which will strengthen the position of the Polish audiovisual sector on the international market, increase the competitiveness of Polish companies operating in the industry, and in the long term attract investment in this sector. This will thus have a positive impact on the widely understood audiovisual creation and many other areas of the economy and social life. The draft law sets out the principles, conditions and modalities for granting and accounting financial support for audiovisual production, without prejudice to the rules for the co-financing of film and other activities in the field of cinematography set out in the Act of 30 June 2005 on cinematography. The general provisions set out in Chapter 1 set out the objectives of the Act and the basic definitions, prejudicing its subject-matter and subject-matter. The aim of introducing a new mechanism for financial support for audiovisual production is primarily to improve the conditions for the functioning of the Polish audiovisual production sector and to create conditions for investing foreign capital in audiovisual productions carried out in the Republic of Poland and to cooperate between Polish and foreign audiovisual producers, as well as to increase the activity of Polish entrepreneurs active in the audiovisual production sector. Financial support should therefore translate into an increase in the international competitiveness of Polish entrepreneurs operating in the sector, which will involve increasing the exploitation of the technical potential of audiovisual production in the territory of the Republic of Poland and increasing the level of professionalism of Polish producers and service providers. As a result, the introduction of the mechanism is intended to benefit from the economic development of the country and the development of culture, as well as the promotion of the image of the Republic of Poland and the Polish cultural heritage in the world. The implementation of the objectives of the bill will be entrusted to the State Film Institute. The tasks of the PSIF related to the implementation of the submitted law will be primarily the granting, payment and settlement of financial support in the form of reimbursement of part of Polish eligible costs. Moreover, PISF's tasks include the collection and analysis of data on the audiovisual market, as well as the promotion of the Polish audiovisual sector and Poland as a place for film production in order to attract foreign producers and investors. The concept of PISF in the area of implementation is based on the best models from other countries of the European Union and the United States, where similar institutions have existed for many years. High Izbo! Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Culture and Media on 23 August this year. The Committee on Culture and Media, following its first reading on 13 September, established an emergency subcommittee to consider this project. On 24 October this year, the committee adopted the report of the subcommittee, introducing 16 substantive amendments to it, which are to a large extent a response to the demands of the social side, and editorial comments from the Legislative Bureau. The Commission has accepted this proposal without objection and recommends that the High House adopt the draft law annexed to the report. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u121	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jacek World, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u122	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law on financial support for audiovisual production. High Seym! A few years ago in Wroclaw he was filming the famous Steven Spielberg. Contrary to the admirations of the then president of the city, this was not the effect of Wrocław's attractiveness as a place of film investment, it was simply one of the last places in Europe which could have been played by Germany from 60 years ago without make-up. After all, it is not about Poland being a museum for filmmakers, it is about being a friendly place, attractive to invest, used in filmmaking, television or video games. This opportunity is given by this Act, which is a little late, because similar solutions have been in operation in many developed countries for years. The Act, as it has been said, lays down the rules of state aid to domestic and foreign companies engaged in audiovisual production or auxiliary, service activities in this area. Importantly, it sets the limit for State expenditure for this purpose. Minimally between 2019 and 2027 it will be PLN 100 million per year. The top spending limit is rising and is expected to reach 266 million in 2027. It is worth noting that 10% is intended for the production of animation. The Act also regulates the award criteria and the rules for verifying the regularity of the spending of this money. We have a lot of bad experience in the past with the control of public spending, which is why this issue takes a special place in the law and has been the subject of the hottest discussions during the subcommittee and committee deliberations. I think we have achieved a good result, that is, a balance between the detail and the tightness of the record, and at the same time we will not tire the producers of excessive bureaucracy. I would add, of course, that the rules for granting public aid are fully in line with EU legislation. During the work on the law, some doubts were raised by entrusting all tasks related to this law to the Polish Film Institute. In my opinion, however, this is the optimal solution. The PISF has experience in financing film production, it has a prepared staff, and with relatively little effort it is able to take on new tasks, and it would seem that setting up a new institution would be a huge exaggeration here. I am convinced that this law and financial support for audiovisual production will allow Poland to join the leading audiovisual production countries, will contribute to the development of this very profitable production, create attractive conditions for Polish and foreign investors, ensure the technological development of the Polish sector, which will help the economic development of the country and finally contribute to the promotion of Poland. This is important - we all know how successful Sandomierz has been since Father Mateusz was filmed there. There is no obstacle in the future that Poland will also become an object of film tourism, as it is happening today with the plans of the Lord of the rings, the games of thrones or other attractive films. Law and Justice, of course, will support this law.

Referring directly to questions, i.e. Before addressing the questions, I would like to thank you for the hard work of the committee, because indeed the bill has been on the committee for a long time and we have worked very hard to find solutions that would fit the social side as much as possible, on the one hand, and on the other hand the possibilities of the state and the ability to control the spending of public money, because it was, apparently, the most difficult issue to solve. Ladies and gentlemen! There is often an argument concerning the possibility of extending the competence of the Polish Film Institute's programme board to exercise supervision of the Institute's director, while in this place I am repeatedly asked that the PISF Board is a programme council. She has competence in the field of e.g. approval of financial statements - there is a possible possibility for it to exercise some control function - while the control and supervisory function is exercised by the Minister of Culture and National Heritage and in accordance with the law and principles of the system that operates in Poland, this function cannot be shared. Of course, Members also have their competences. Your marshal raised the question of whether there would be information on how the money was spent, etc. Of course, because the Polish Film Institute now introduces a transparent system, i.e. all persons, all entities that have received any funding will be posted and are already posted on websites. So not only will you be able to see the functioning of the new system after a while, but you can also keep track of who gets the money and what rules. As far as issues related to supporting young artists are concerned, it is now that the director of the Polish Film Institute has introduced a new mechanism for financing debuts not only strictly, because anyone of all ages can debut, but above all young artists who will create new works, e.g. right after graduation or as part of a diploma. The Minister of Culture has allocated additional money to this issue for the Polish Film Institute so that this year it can be launched with this program. As far as film storage is concerned, the Act says that copies must be sent to the Polish Film Institute, but this is not a waiver of the obligation to transfer copies to the National Film Library - the Audiovisual Institute. This obligation still exists because these copies are stored for two different purposes: on the one hand, for the preservation of heritage in the National Film Library, and on the other hand for purposes, let's call it economical and economic accounting. In this respect, the Polish Film Institute will keep these copies. These copies can also be brought on some medium and stored on servers, so this is not a problem for the Polish Film Institute. [[Margaret Kidawa-Błońska: So there will be an archive at the institute?]] Each institution has an archive, so to some extent this archive will be there, but in general all copies are in accordance with the Act in the National Film Library. And when it comes to the system itself, will there be someone who will judge content in some sense, in the system that we have now, there will be no such possibility. We're gonna have to pass a cultural test. In the cultural test, which will be an annex to the bill... That is, in the regulation, all the rules will be described in detail, all the points to be met so that the system will act automatically so that money can be applied for, for example. whether Polish artists will participate in this film, whether there will be Polish landscapes, or Polish musicians will create music for this film, etc., etc. There's gonna be a whole series of points. The draft regulation is available and can be consulted. Indeed, as Mr Mieszkowski rightly pointed out, the space of art is a democratic space and any creator can create the content he likes as long as it is legal, i.e. does not violate general principles of law, does not offend anyone, does not promote e.g. communist or fascist content, etc., etc. So by reducing it, in Poland you can make any film, even the dumbest, everyone has the right to do it. [[Member Iwona Śledzińska-Katarasinska: And that happens.]] It happens, unfortunately. As far as the issue of this mechanism is concerned, it is not a mechanism that directly supports cultural issues, although it affects culture. I'm talking about it because here was the issue of why not at the beginning, but at the end. At the beginning of the Polish Film Institute gives money, it is possible to obtain money, because that is the Polish system, that someone is applying to the Polish Film Institute, fills in applications, then the films are evaluated, whether they are suitable for further production, for further proceding, then at the Polish Film Institute there is a recommended amount to support and this amount is awarded by the director of the Polish Film Institute, then the film is produced, and only after the production of this film can you possibly apply for the so-called. Cash-rebate mechanism. This mechanism is an economic mechanism, not a cultural mechanism, i.e. On the one hand, in order to promote Polish culture, we allow you to reduce the risk of economic activity, which is very risky when it comes to culture, because sometimes you can create a really great work that is simply not going to take up and earn money in the world. In order not to look far away: when the film was created, the Android Hunter was a flop in the United States, only after 10 years became very popular and only further versions began to actually earn. So sometimes you can just miss the work into your epoch, but you can't make the creators afraid to create it, or else we'd reduce innovation. So this is a pro-innovation mechanism, when it comes to what we are creating, what we are debating today. It is a parallel system and the Polish Film Institute has the competence to operate both systems within one institution. There is no need to create more than a measure of existence, to allocate new money to create new directors, new deputies, new accountants, etc., etc. If this is all in one institution, then we can rely on it as a matter of fact in the world. To operate this system is to be additionally employed, it seems, only a few people. Please note that the system is as automatic as possible, i.e. that those who will be employed to handle this system will be more concerned with formal issues than with any detailed and profound analysis. We hope that the multiplier effect will also work here, i.e. We assume that about PLN 300 million will be paid for each PLN 100 million that will be spent under this system. So we take this not as a simple transfer from the budget, but as a certain investment. This investment worked well in most countries that introduced such a system, increased competitiveness, made us more familiar with non-American cinemas. If we go in, for example. on Netflix, we will be able to notice that there are productions from different parts of the world, e.g. more and more Korean productions, including that's because there's a similar system operating there as well. We want as many Polish productions as possible to be on such international services, so that we can also show our culture, our capabilities and our actors, and, as you mentioned, build our soft power. Therefore, there is nothing left for me to do but ask Members to adopt this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u140	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Sejm sent this proposal to the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development. The Commission at its meeting on 22 October this year. This project was considered. The committee's report is in print 2924. The proposed amendments to the Law of 21 December 2000 on Technical Dosor are intended to bring the provisions of the Law on Technical Dosore into line with the current legal situation in other areas affecting the performance of the relevant technical supervision units and to increase the safety in the operation of technical equipment subject to technical supervision. These amendments aim to clarify the rules and improve the selected areas of the technical supervision system while reducing administrative barriers for entrepreneurs. The proposed amendments also implement the recommendations made following the inspection of the Supreme Audit Chamber at the Technical Supervision Office. 30 amendments are made to the bill, which the committee has examined in detail. During the discussion, a number of legislative amendments were tabled, which the overwhelming majority of the committee adopted. The only substantive amendment was tabled by representatives of the Polish Liquid Gas Organisation, an amendment which, following the discussion, the committee rejected by a majority as going beyond the submission of the amendments under consideration. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development recommends that the High Sejm adopt the proposed amendments to the Technical Dosor Act. Thank you.

Physiotherapists are people with medical education, specialists who are perfectly qualified and can help patients recover. I am therefore extremely surprised that until recently, the profession of physiotherapist was not regulated in Polish legislation, moreover, attempts to regulate it aroused considerable opposition from certain environments. Only three years ago, President Andrzej Duda signed the physio therapist's law. The law specified in detail who the physiotherapist is, what rights and obligations he has, what is the scope of his competence. However, the President's signature did not mean the conclusion of the physiotherapist case, as some groups were still negative for the proposed solutions and sought to maintain the status quo in the field of the physiotherapist profession. In February 2016, a group of Members of Law and Justice submitted a draft law proposing an extension of the entry into force of the Act signed by President Andrzej Duda for an additional 18 months. This bill would probably never enter into force. This project was postponed for an indefinite period of time. We remember the numerous manifestations of patients and physiotherapists before the Sejm. Thanks to the great determination of physiotherapists, the law, which they have been waiting almost 28 years, entered into force on the initially proposed date. Although physiotherapist regulations have been in force for more than two years, it turns out that some of the solutions introduced are detrimental to those practicing physiotherapists. This applies to regulations relating to the form of pursuit of the physiotherapist's profession. In view of the lack of possibility to pursue a profession in the form of individual and group professional practices, this situation is detrimental to physiotherapists who have provided health services in person, often only at the place of call or in another medicinal product. Today's bill aims to improve adverse rules which, in my view, are incompatible with the principles of equality and social justice. The major change proposed in the project concerns the introduction of a certain possibility - physiotherapists can pursue a profession in business not only as a therapeutic entity but also as an individual or group physiotherapist practice. This form of benefit reduces organisational requirements compared to medicinal products and is therefore easier to organise as well as much cheaper. In conclusion, I would like to point out that this law expects tens of thousands of physiotherapists, and in order not to cause any problems related to the loss of the right to pursue the profession, it is necessary that the rules enter into force at the latest on 29 November of this year, i.e. in 3 weeks' time. Therefore, on behalf of the physiotherapists and their patients, please take immediate steps in the legislative procedure. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u167	Thank you very much. Please take the floor of the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Health, Mrs Józef Szczurek-Żelazko.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u168	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! At the beginning, I would like to thank all Members of the various clubs very warmly for their positive opinions on this project. Indeed, it is expected by the physiotherapists' environment. The change was prepared at the Ministry of Health to meet all physiotherapists practicing in the field of health care, but not only in education or in the field of social policy. With regard to your individual doubts and questions, I would like to begin by correcting some of Mr Sachaika's statements, which stated that the possible absence of this change could cause the physiotherapists to lose their right to pursue their profession. There is absolutely no such threat. The absence of this regulation could cause problems with the profession, but the rights of the profession were not and are not at risk. As regards Mr Pyzik's question regarding information for the physiotherapists' environment, with the transmission of messages about this law, I would like to stress that this change, like all the changes concerning the pursuit of the physiotherapist's profession, which we are constantly agreeing with the physiotherapists' environment and with the professional self-government of the physiotherapists with whom we cooperate, is still discussed and agreed with this environment. Of course, information about this is provided through the media, but it is also committed by the professional self-government. Of course, the Minister of Health will give such information through the usual channels. Regarding the number of physiotherapists employed in the system, ladies and gentlemen, since we did not have physiotherapists registers and there was no law on local government, they were educated in various systems, so far we have not really had information about the number of physiotherapists practicing in Poland in the field of health care or in other areas. And it is this law that gives us the opportunity to record this phenomenon. It introduces the need to register individual practices, group practices, as well as to issue the right to pursue the profession by the physiotherapists' self-government, which gives us the opportunity to precisely balance the number of physiotherapists practicing in our country. Currently they are already in the register about 60,000. These are the physiotherapists who have been granted the right to pursue the profession, and it should be presumed that this number provides services because persons without the right to pursue the profession cannot pursue health care benefits. However, as for the number of physiotherapists who have left our country, neither do we have such data. As in this, no one keeps such records for other medical professions. In addition, physiotherapists, even those who have registered in a professional self-government, are not obliged to report the fact that they cease their occupation in the Republic of Poland. Therefore, we do not have such a register. This law makes it possible to assess all matters relating to the pursuit of the physiotherapist's profession, but also those relating to the quality of the services provided, under the responsibility of the professional self-government of physiotherapists, and on this basis it will be possible to assess possible needs with regard to changes in the training process, professional training or other working conditions and pay of physiotherapists. There was a question about pay. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to stress that the current government, the Minister of Health, has introduced regulations that guarantee a systematic increase in salaries in the area of health care for all workers, including physiotherapists. The Basic Minimum Compensation Act provides for an annual increase in basic salary for physiotherapists depending on their level of education. Those with higher medical education have the minimum basic salary indicated therein. Those with higher education or specialisation will also be automatically re-skilled to a different level of remuneration. As I said, this bill guarantees an annual systematic increase in healthcare salaries. As you may remember, because you voted in favour of this law, the government will spend almost PLN 16 billion in this type of regulation by 2021. These measures will affect the health care system, individual employees, professionals, including physiotherapists. On the other hand, there are, of course, ongoing discussions with the environments, representatives of trade union organisations or professional authorities on the conditions of the profession, including financing conditions. We're open. The Ministry of Health has a dialogue. Such discussions are also ongoing in the Social Dialogue Council, which is the body competent to settle remuneration issues. That's where the decisions are made. An example of this is the amendment of the law that I was talking about this year, which was due to the expectations of the environment. Finally, I would like to thank you very much for your positive opinion on this project. [[Applause]]

In fact, physiotherapists often perform their duties in many different institutions or even go to patients for home visits. Until now, there has been no such judicial review in Poland. The Court of Justice of the European Union held in its judgment of 13 December 2017 that Article 4 (2) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 should apply. Article 32 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Visa Code must be interpreted as meaning that, in connection with the content of the Article. 47 The Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union requires the Member States of the Union to establish an appeal procedure against a decision refusing to issue a visa or a decision determining the revocation of a visa or the cancellation of a visa decision. The government project is fulfilling this obligation by introducing a judicial appeal procedure. The person concerned will have the opportunity to request a review of the case to the consul, and only after using this measure will he be able to lodge a complaint with the competent administrative court in whose jurisdiction the office serving the minister responsible for foreign affairs has its seat. This court of jurisdiction of the first instance will be the Provincial Administrative Court in Warsaw and, as appropriate, in the event of the lodging of an appeal at the second instance - the Supreme Administrative Court. At its meeting of 6 November this year. Two legislative amendments have been made: Article. 1 in points (a) and (b) of the Act and in Article. 2, as well as two substantives. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 4 introduced a 2-month period for the entry into force of the Act from the date of its publication. In the original text, that law was to enter into force on 1 January 2019. This change concerning the new vacathio legis also results in a second substantive amendment in the form of an amendment to the article. 3 and a new limit on the maximum expenditure of the State budget resulting from the entry into force of that law. This limit has been set for the years 2019 to 2028. High Izbo! Both committees are requesting the adoption of a bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u184	Thank you very much. Mrs Elizabeth Stępień, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u185	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It is my pleasure to present the position of the Modern Club regarding print number 2908. It is the Law of the Administrative Courts. The draft law provides for a revision of the provisions of the Law of 30 August 2002 concerning the control of visa decisions by the consul. The government proposal proposes to add a regulation which guarantees the applicant to submit an appeal against the decision of the consul to the competent administrative court on the issue of visas. Discussion of the project is also a consequence of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union. On 13 December 2017, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European The Court held that the procedure for issuing visas by a consul at a certain stage of the procedure should guarantee an appeal to the court. The European Commission informed that, in the event of failure to act on the Polish side, a decision would be taken to initiate the infringement procedure. I believe that this is the reason for this urgency in the adoption of this proposal. I would just like to add that the draft law provides for a revision of the regulation concerning the control of visa decisions by the consul by guaranteeing the possibility of bringing an appeal before the court in cases of refusal of visas. Until now, the administrative courts have not been competent to issue visas by consuls, with the exception of visas issued to a foreigner who is a member of the family of a citizen of a Member State of the European Union. In fact, this project is intended to extend the scope of the cognition of administrative courts also to Schengen visa cases. Therefore, the voivodship administrative courts will have jurisdiction in cases of complaints about refusal of a visa, the revocation or cancellation of Schengen visas and the activities of the consul in this respect. The projector assumes that the Voivodeship Administrative Court in Warsaw will be competent in these cases because of its experience. The proposal proposes a solution whereby a party cannot exercise its right to appeal to the administrative court, but before submitting an application to the court, it must submit a request to the consul for review. And here we should add that there are four pages of this act, namely the Consul of the Republic of Poland, the Minister of Foreign Affairs, the applicant for Schengen visas, as well as the Voivodship Administrative Court. Looking generally at all these records, which are in fact intended to safeguard the transparency of issuing visas, modern is in favour of this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u195	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the law on proceedings before administrative courts, print No. 2908. This proposal assumes that the decision to refuse, revoke or revoke a Schengen visa may be brought before the Voivodship Administrative Court. The draft amendment was prepared to adapt the Polish legal order to the ruling of the Court of Justice of the EU and the recommendations of the European Commission to the Polish government. In 2017, the Council The Court pointed out that Member States, in the light of the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the EU, are required to establish the right to appeal to the court against decisions to refuse, revoke or revoke a Schengen visa. The infringement procedure was initiated in 2013 and the European Commission did not accept the argument of the Polish Government that the Polish rules, providing for the possibility of requesting a review of the case by the consul, are in line with EU law. According to the draft complaint against the decisions of the consuls, he is to consider the WSA in Warsaw, which, as stated in the explanatory memorandum, gathered the greatest experience in these matters and developed a common judicial line. In the proceedings before the Voivodship Administrative Court on Schengen visas for foreigners, the Minister for Foreign Affairs is to conduct proceedings for the consul. The consul will, in turn, be required to draw up a description of the facts, indicate the relevant circumstances of the case and to order the case file and to draw up a preliminary position on the complaint. The Consul will prepare a set of documentation and a description of the facts of the case within approx. It shall forward the file to the Foreign Minister without undue delay. The Minister shall refer the complaint lodged through the Consul to the NSA together with the file of the case and the reply to the complaint within 60 days of its receipt by the Consul. It was assumed that only after exercising the right to review the case by the consul would the party be able to lodge a complaint with the provincial administrative court. If the party fails to submit a request for review, it shall not be entitled to lodge a complaint with the NSA. According to analyses by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, a decision concerning the Schengen visa is amended in approximately 30% of applications for review after the applicant has submitted additional information or documents. The aim is to ensure that the audit carried out by the WSA concerns only those cases where the complainant had the opportunity to present his arguments at an earlier stage of the proceeding and to exercise that right. High Izbo! In accordance with applicable legal, Polish and European standards, everyone deserves the right to complain and appeal against decisions of state authorities. In accordance with the judgment of the European Court of Justice, a Member State is required to establish an appeal procedure against a decision refusing to issue a visa whose detailed rules fall within the legal order of each Member State, while respecting the principles of equivalence and effectiveness. This procedure must guarantee a judicial appeal at a certain stage of the procedure. Today's project will adapt to these standards the procedure for applying for a Schengen visa in Polish diplomatic institutions abroad. In view of this, we welcome him, we consider him worthy of further work in committees.

Another major, important change is changing the POLADA task directory. It will be able to conduct business activities, provide services for new entities, not only for Polish sports unions. Another change is the change in the qualification requirements for controllers. They will not be able to be members of Polish sports unions. I think these are the most important changes. Poland will thus be entitled to speak at meetings of the World Anti-Doping Agency. The Modern Posel Club supports the government's proposed amendments to this law. I ask Mr Andrzej Matusiewicz to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u219	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I present a report by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications on the Senate bill amending the Civil Procedure Code Act, Seym Printing No. 2769 and 2894. The Commission considered this draft law at its meeting on 4 October 2018. It's so-called. Judgemental initiative of the Senate, related to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 3 October 2017, sygn. Act SK 31/15, finding non-compliance with Article. 778 Code of Civil Procedure, Art. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. It was a so-called judgment. range. The Court held that in its Article. 778 The Civil Procedure Code, in so far as it allows the court to issue a enforcement title against a public company, a declaration of enforceability against a former partner of that company, who is no longer a partner at the time of the initiation of proceedings in the case in which the enforcement title was issued against a public company, is incompatible with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution - the right to court, and Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitution - the point is that the law must not close anybody's judicial path to the investigation of violated freedoms or rights. In the statement of reasons for the judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal stated that the former shareholder did not take part in the judicial exploratory proceedings but only in a limited manner in the clause procedure which is already pending after the finality of the judgment was established. Current content of the article. 778 The Civil Procedure Code reads: The title of enforcement issued against a public company, partner company, limited partnership or limited partnership, the court gives a declaration of enforceability against a partner who is liable without limitation by all his assets for the company's obligations if the execution against the company proves ineffective, as well as when it is evident that the execution will be ineffective. The Senate amendment in the article. 778 c.p.c. the following second sentence is added: This does not apply to a person who was no longer a partner at the time of the initiation of the proceedings in the case in which the enforcement title was issued against the company. The draft law contains a transitional provision in Article. 2 which was supplemented at the committee meeting on 4 October 2018 by the addition of the words "and unfinished'. This is about proceedings initiated and not completed. Before the date of entry into force of the amended law, the text of the article shall apply. 778 Code of Civil Procedure - as in the novel. The period of vacathio legis is 14 days from the date of publication of the law, i.e. it is a minimum period. The Commission calls for the adoption of a draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u220	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marek Ast, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.

The second sentence reads: "This does not apply to a person who, at the time of the initiation of the proceedings in the case in which the enforcement title was issued against the company, was no longer a partner of the company, that protection of the former partner is possible, may defend himself by judicial means against, in his view, unjustified claims of creditors. This project in this shape will, on the one hand, in accordance with the provisions of the Civil Code, be able to claim the satisfaction of our claims, and on the other hand, after changes to the Civil Procedure Code, this possibility is taken away from us. Therefore, when we see here the need to amend other provisions, the need to clarify them, we need to think deeply about whether to support this proposal. Thank you. [[Applause]]

And I have to say that I have a doubt here, because, of course, we can say that a creditor can claim satisfaction through civil proceedings against a partner who is no longer a partner, but let's just say that this is also a gateway to creating a certain circulation of bankruptcy reports: I am no longer in this company, and to avert liability. Therefore, the danger is that the project will also cause the so-called. fictitious or combination bankruptcy, and we must have one thing in mind - and it seems to me that the PiS here does not notice - that what else is a civil sentence, on the basis of which the obligation must be covered, and what else is its execution. Well, we all know very well that receiving a judgment is not equivalent to receiving money. And that gives us time, opportunity, and we open the other gate. So that I think we will still have to think about this too, because there are great doubts here that this will not lead to abuse. Thank you. [[Applause]]

I ask Mr Czesław Sobierajski to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u237	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Foreign Affairs a report on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Amendment to the Montreal Protocol on Substances that Deplete the Ozone Layer, drawn up in Montreal on 16 September 1987, adopted in Kigali on 15 October 2016, prints No 2879 and 2972. The draft law deals with the creation of a framework and principles for the joint action of the various countries to protect the ozone layer, including amendments to the Montreal Protocol on Substances that Deplete the ozone layer. And a few details. The Montreal Protocol on Substances that Deplete the Ozone Layer, drawn up on 16 September 1987, is the consequence of the two years previously drawn up Vienna Convention for the Protection of the Ozone Layer. Poland ratified the Montreal Protocol in 1990. The obligations arising from the Montreal Protocol for developed countries, including Poland, mainly concern the total reduction of production and consumption of a number of substances that deplete the ozone layer, so-called. controlled substances of which approximately 40 are present. In addition, the provisions of the Protocol introduce mechanisms for market surveillance of controlled substances in the form of annual reporting and licensing of imports and exports of controlled substances, including mixtures containing them. In Polish law, the above issues are comprehensively regulates the Act of 15 May 2015 on substances that deplete the ozone layer and on certain fluorinated greenhouse gases. The amendments from Kigali were agreed at a meeting of the parties to the Kigali Protocol in Rwanda in October 2016 and will enter into force on 1 January 2019. This is another revision of the Montreal Protocol. All the amendments previously adopted have been ratified by Poland. The Kigali amendments have introduced the following additions to the protocol. The list of controlled substances has been extended by 19 substances commonly used as substitutes for ozone-depleting substances. Schedules for the reduction of HFCs have been introduced for developed countries and developing countries. The obligation to submit annual reports to the Ozone Secretariat for reports on the production, import and export of HFC substances has been extended. The obligation to import and export controlled substances to HFC substances has been extended. The withdrawal of HFCs in developing countries was covered by the Multilateral Protocol Fund. It should be stressed that Poland, as a Member State of the European Union, fulfils all the obligations relating to the withdrawal of HFCs resulting from European Union legislation, which are already more restrictive than those arising from the Kigali amendments, because ratification of amendments will not result in any legislative or additional obligations for entrepreneurs for Poland. The committees therefore request the adoption of the law unanimously. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u238	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Barbara Dziuk, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u239	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of Law and Justice vis-à-vis the Government's bill of ratification of the Amendment to the Montreal Protocol on substances that deplete the ozone layer, drawn up in Montreal on 16 September 1987, adopted in Kigali on 15 October 2016. The Kigali amendments to the Montreal Protocol provide climate protection against hydrofluorocarbons, i.e. gases with a strong greenhouse effect, and are used to reduce their production and consumption. These gases, when entering the atmosphere, cause climate change, contributing to global warming and are far more harmful than carbon dioxide. They are used, among others. in refrigerators, air conditioners, solvents and aerosols. The current system of international cooperation on the protection of the ozone layer is based on the 1985 Vienna Convention and the 1987 Montreal Protocol, which concerned the withdrawal from production and consumption of substances threatening the ozone layer. Poland joined the Vienna Convention in 1990. We ratified all amendments to the Montreal Protocol in addition to the amendments on ozone-depleting substances in Montreal in September 1987, adopted in Kigali on 15 October 2016. The amendments from Kigali were adopted at the XXVIII Meeting of the Parties to the Montreal Protocol when proposals were negotiated for the introduction into the Montreal Protocol of a timetable for phasing out the use and production of these gases. On behalf of Poland, the agreed position within the European Union was presented by the European Commission and the Presidency of the Council of the European Union. The purpose of the amendments from Kigali to the Montreal Protocol is to continue Poland's participation in the Montreal Protocol and to enable the European Union to join the amendments from Kigali. The main amendment introduced by the amendments to the Montreal Protocol is the extension of the list of substances subject to the Montreal Protocol. The changes resulting from the amendments from Kigali have already been included in European Union law and Polish law together with the implementing acts, so their ratification does not require additional legislative action, nor does they have any social or economic impact on Poland, as their provisions are now being fulfilled under the existing national and European Union legislation. Ratification of amendments from Kigali by the Polish government will strengthen our credibility in the European Union and in the global forum in the field of ozone and climate protection cooperation. This is particularly important in 2018, when Poland covers the Presidency 24. Conference of the Parties to the United Nations Framework Convention [[♪ The bell ♪]] on climate change COP24, and this will certainly contribute to strengthening the positive image of Poland.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u250	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u251	The text of the Act includes the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the Amendment to the Montreal Protocol on substances that deplete the ozone layer, drawn up in Montreal on 16 September 1987, adopted in Kigali on 15 October 2016. The Kigali amendments to the Montreal Protocol on the withdrawal of the production and consumption of ozone-depleting substances aim at protecting the climate from gases with a strong greenhouse effect - hydrofluorocarbons, i.e. fluorinated greenhouse gases, in short HFCs. HFC gases are present in equipment and everyday products such as refrigerators, air conditioners, foams and aerosols. Amendments are expected to directly affect the limits by 85% emissions of potentially emitted HFC gases expressed in CO2 eq (carbon dioxide equivalent) by reducing their production and consumption, which is expected to reduce global warming by up to 0.5°C at the end of the century. In addition, these amendments support the objectives of the Paris Agreement, adopted at the Paris climate conference in December 2015 by 195 countries, whose long-term objective is to keep the global average temperature rise well below 2°C above pre-industrial levels. The Parties to the Montreal Protocol on substances that deplete the ozone layer are 197. The commitments resulting from the amendments in question from Kigali are already contained in Regulation (EU) No 517/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on fluorinated greenhouse gases and repealing Regulation (EC) No For developed countries, i.e. EU Member States, the withdrawal of HFCs under these amendments will not go beyond much more restrictive EU legislation. Therefore, for Poland, this ratification is a kind of formality, which also depends on the full participation of the EU in the Montreal Protocol. The provisions of the Montreal Protocol are so important and necessary that compliance with their guidelines by the obliged states will result in the full recovery of the ozone layer by the middle of this century. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, the costs which may result from the adoption of this law concern only the increase of the contribution of the Republic of Poland as a party to the Montreal Protocol (Multilateral Fund) since 2021 in connection with the extension of the catalogue of substances to be withdrawn under the Montreal Protocol. The Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of the bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u275	The existence of this bilateral agreement between Poland and the Czech Republic is contested by the European Commission due to the non-compliance with EU law, as is contested by the existence of all other agreements to promote and protect mutual investment between EU Member States. The Court of Justice of the European Union also expressed its opinion on this issue, which, in response to the question referred for a preliminary ruling, stated that the investment promotion and protection agreements were incompatible with EU law. These agreements were intended to provide full protection for both the Polish and the other parties and the possibility of redress against an international investment tribunal in the event of violation of the rights of any of the parties to the agreement. The protection of investment through international arbitration was intended to be a kind of fuse for both foreign investors in Poland and for the entry into the free market game of the Polish entrepreneur. The European Commission has serious concerns that agreements for the promotion and mutual protection of investment, including the above-mentioned agreements, as well as their individual provisions, are in line with or may conflict with the provisions of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, in particular the principles of freedom of movement of capital, freedom of establishment and freedom to provide services and the fundamental principles of EU law, such as the principle of primacy, unity, efficiency and security of EU law. The European Commission also has reservations about the settlement of investment disputes governed by bilateral investment agreements, including those mentioned above, which, according to the European Commission, seems to be contrary to the exclusive competence of EU courts. The termination of the agreement will lead to compliance with EU law with relations between Poland and the Czech Republic in the field of economic cooperation. At Community level, termination of agreements should not have political consequences. Poland and the Czech Republic were invited by the European Commission to conclude intra-EU BIT agreements. The agreement will not have negative social consequences, on the contrary, it is expected that it will be welcomed by public criticism in Poland of intra-EU BIT agreements and international investment arbitration.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u278	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Mr Tadeusz Wozniak, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u279	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I was honoured to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the Government bill amending the Police Act and some other laws, prints No 2828 and 2962. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the bill, recognising that it is important and aims to increase the security of both Polish citizens and other people residing in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Terrorism is today an extremely dangerous form of political and ideological warfare, which must be countered by all possible forms within the legal limits using the best-fits-all services and the best-trained. The bill responds to a real need for harmonisation in the law of anti-terrorism and counter-terrorism concepts and the actual creation of a counter-terrorism service as a service compatible with other services, both national and international. The newly established service is expected to be responsible for counter-terrorism activities within the meaning of the Anti-Terrorism Act of 10 June 2016 and to support the activities of police agencies under conditions of special threat or requiring the use of specialised forces and resources and specialised tactics of action. The proposed amendments are foreseen within the current permanent status of the Office for Anti-Terrorism Operations of the Police Headquarters and the independent units of the Anti-Terrorist Police. In order to ensure historical continuity, the abbreviation BOA from the name of the former Anti-Terror Operations Office was left as the name of the Central Counter-Terrorist Police Unit. In addition to the BOA, there will be independent police counter-terrorist units. The superior of the BOA will be chief of police, who will also appoint the commander of the BOA and, at his request, the deputy. The commanders of the independent counter-terrorist divisions will be appointed from among the counter-terrorism officers by the competent police commanders or the police capital commander. It should be pointed out that, in order to harmonise the functioning of the system and the organisation of independent sub-groups of counter-terrorism police, the project provides for the allocation of the BOA's competence to coordinate, prepare and implement counter-terrorism activities of the police. At the same time, a solution is envisaged to ensure that counter-terrorism activities will be carried out by the BOA and the police's own counter-terrorism units with priority before other actions in order to ensure effective response to terrorist events. The proposal also provides for the possibility to withdraw from compliance with the rules and principles of health and safety of the service, with all safeguards available under the conditions in question, if, in the course of carrying out the duties in circumstances justified by the need for a higher level of necessity, a police officer in the counter-terrorism service finds that there is a likelihood of saving human health or life or reversing a threat to the security of the State. This provision will allow the use of all available means to save lives by counter-terrorism police officers. A similar solution already exists in the laws governing the functioning of services such as Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Central Anti-corruption Office, Military Counter-intelligence Service and Military Intelligence Service. As a result of the work of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, including the Extraordinary Sub-Committee, the project has been slightly improved by introducing several legislative and more detailed amendments. In its present form, it is fully deserved to be passed by the High Sejm. At this point I would like to thank all the members of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the members of the extraordinary subcommittee for their substantive and consistent cooperation in order to present this report to the High House in the interests of the security of our citizens and other people residing in the Republic of Poland, the alignment of Polish legislation with international and national law, including the unification and proper application of the concepts of counter-terrorism in relation to the law on anti-terrorism, in which the concepts of counter-terrorism and counter-terrorism are not strictly synonymous. I would also like to thank Minister Jarosław Zieliński and those accompanying him for the substantive reasons which have fully convinced all Members involved in the submission. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft contained in the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, print No 2962, and declares the vote in favour of its adoption, calling on all Members for a similar position, because it is in the interest of Poland, our citizens and others. [[♪ The bell ♪]] people who want to live in harmony with the law and feel safe. Thank you. [[Applause]]
