ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u300	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed to the examination of item 28 of the agenda: First reading of the bill presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the Civil Code Act (Print No 2876). I ask the Deputy Head of the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland of Mr.Paweł Mucha to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u301	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a bill to amend the Civil Code Act. The novel is short and consists of three articles. The first concerns the repeal of Article. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code of 23 April 1964. This provision contains an exception to the exception to interest claims on late interest. According to Art. 482 § 1 of the Civil Code interest on late interest may be requested only from the date on which the parties have applied for it or have given their consent to add it to the debt amount after its formation. In turn, the paragraph 2 in question, which is to be repealed by this amendment, provides that this restriction does not apply to long-term loans granted by credit institutions. The second article of the novel determines the possibility of charging interest on interest under contracts on the date of the entry into force of the law which I am presenting. The amendment is intended to cover not only contracts concluded after its introduction but all credit agreements, long-term loans and loans concluded before its entry into force in respect of interest due after its entry into force. This provision therefore provides that the existing provisions will apply to interest due for periods which ended before the date of entry into force of the amendment. The third article of the proposed amendment concerns vacathio legis. The applicant decided to extend the period of vacathio legis from the usual 14 days to 30 days due to the importance of this amendment and its importance for the financial market. As you know, anatolysm means taking interest on late interest. In other words, a debtor who does not regularly pay his debts to the creditor may be punished in such a way that he is charged additional interest on late interest. Anatolycism as such is prohibited in Polish law. There are three exceptions to the ban on anatomy. The first is to bring an action. At the time of the action, the principal sum together with outstanding interest shall capitalise and the court may also be required to charge interest for outstanding interest. The second concerns a situation where the parties, after the backlog, agree to an interest addition. The third relates to a situation where a long-term loan is granted by a credit institution. The provision on the prohibition of anatolycism and exceptions to it was introduced by the Civil Code in 1965 and is still functioning unchanged today. When the Civil Code entered into force, credit institutions were the Central Bank, special and industry banks, credit cooperatives and savings banks. Long-term loans, on the other hand, could only be granted by special banks. And the content of that provision, aimed at safeguarding the economic interests of state-owned entities, has remained unchanged until now. Despite its inadequacy, since in the meantime there has been a change in the system, it has not yet been changed. Due to serious interpretational doubts, both as regards the term "credit institution' and as regards the definition of "long-term loan', this provision is in practice a source of many controversy. As defined in the Banking Law, credit institutions other than cooperative banks are also savings and credit banks. However, the legal doctrine considers that the provision to be repealed by this amendment does not apply to them. The same is true of the concept of a long-term loan, which is a liquid, inconspicuous concept and raises doubts. A large part of the legal community recognizes that this concept includes credit, but some experts express a different opinion. It is also doubtful what period of contract makes the contract long-term. True, some point out that this is a matter of at least one year, but that statement is not based on any legal provision. In general law, it was assumed that a credit institution should be identified with the bank in accordance with the provisions on the prohibition of anatolycism. In turn the long-term loan began to be identified with the loan. A similar interpretation is followed not only by banks and doctrines, but also by court. On the other hand, it should be mentioned here that interest on interest also raises doubts of interpretation under tax law. The question arose as to whether capitalised interest constituted tax revenue at the date of capitalization of interest or at the time of actual payment. The provision, which may seem quite harmless today due to low inflation and thus low interest rates on loans and loans, is, however, a very strong tool for the borrower, who ceases to pay off the debt related e.g. with a mortgage or a credit card. A bank without any judicial review may then charge additional interest on outstanding interest where it accounts for a large part of the debt. In addition, the situation may become dangerous for borrowers in the event of a significant change in the monetary policy of the State or another financial crisis. The regulation that is proposed will be very practical and is a legal proposal which responds to the expectations of consumers, businesses and is undoubtedly beneficial for them. First of all, any newly concluded credit agreement or loan will not be subject to the risk of a bank charging criminal interest in the event of default until the parties have brought an action for it or have recognised it. Secondly, in the case of previously concluded credit agreements or loans as of the date of entry into force of this Regulation, credit institutions will not be able to charge further interest on late interest without earlier bringing an action or a concerted statement by the parties in this respect. The analysis carried out at the President's Office confirms that this change of law is a change which is both acceptable and justified, and necessary, and, as I said, favorable from the perspective of debtors. They do not rely on the truth or are not relevant to such arguments that maintaining this provision would allegedly be in the interest of debtors. It should be noted in advance that it would be difficult to take this hypothesis seriously when it is not clear how negative the proposed initiative could affect the situation of customers. Possibly, the banks can be expected to sue, which will result in additional legal costs on the part of the defendants, but as I have already said, banks can now collect interest on interest without filing a lawsuit without any risk associated with this type of activity. The proposed amendment will require the bank to go to court in order to be able to charge interest on late interest. It should be borne in mind that there is also an institution for the recognition of an action in civil proceedings and that no costs for the debtor must be associated with such a situation. There is also no risk that interest will be calculated to such an extent that it will exceed the value of the collateral, which is particularly important in the case of loans to foreign currencies or secured by mortgages. The borrower also gets a long time in which he can take steps to find other financial sources to repay the loan, and is not surprised by the bank's one-sided calculation of criminal interest. It took several months to work on this project. He received a positive opinion from five institutions: the Ministry of Justice, the Ministry of Finance, the Financial Ombudsman, which I particularly stress, the National Bank of Poland and the Office of the Financial Supervision Commission. In the course of this work, an inter-temporal provision was also introduced as a response to the doubts surrounding the application of the amendment to existing loans. Any question of interpretation linked to the genesis of the deleted provision and the unwarranted position of the banks in legal disputes had decided that President Andrzej Duda had decided to propose a legislative initiative to delete the article I mentioned. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code Act. This law will undoubtedly improve the situation of borrowers in their relationship with banks and is one of the many steps we need to take to achieve this objective. I would like to remind you that the President of the Republic of Poland also directed initiatives to protect the weaker side of relations, contracts, loans or loans, including in the context of foreign currency contracts. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to ask for a positive assessment of the project submitted and for it to be adopted as proposed. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u302	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marek Ast, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u303	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the presidential draft law amending the Civil Code Act in print No 2876. The project, as the Minister explained in the explanatory memorandum, concerns the liquidation of the preference of credit institutions in the calculation of interest on late interest pursuant to Article. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code. In Polish law, the general rule of civil law is that interest on late interest is prohibited, so-called. A ban on anatolysm, but there are exceptions to it. These two, three exceptions are also governed by law: two in such a situation where interest is capitalised on the basis of either the agreement of the parties, or after an outstanding claim has been brought, and this third situation arises from Article. 284 § 2 of the Civil Code and gives this privileged position to credit institutions. Indeed, this provision was brought into legal order during the right period of the past, i.e. under the conditions of the socialist economy. During the whole period of the Third Republic it has not been changed. There is no question that it favours institutions that are stronger than borrowers, borrowers, i.e. mainly banks that have a number of instruments that allow the recovery of outstanding debts. This additional advantage in the form of the possibility of charging interest on late interest seems indeed unjustified. The presidential proposal goes in the right direction, it will have good results in the form of, above all, strengthening borrowers, borrowers. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u321	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats our position on the bill submitted by the President of the Republic of Poland amending the Civil Code Act (Print No 2876). The project envisages eliminating interest rates, i.e. the so-called interest rates. Anatolysm. The proposal aims to remove from our legal system the exception to the ban on anatomy contained in the article. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code. It is therefore a question of eliminating the unjustified preference of credit institutions in the calculation of interest on late interest on the basis of this provision. Currently, the Civil Code provides for three exceptions to the prohibition of anatolycism. The first concerns the case where the parties have agreed, after the interest has arisen, to capitalise on them. The second exception concerns the capital injections made after an outstanding interest claim has been brought. The third exception is subjective and refers to credit institutions. According to Art. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code is allowed to collect interest on late interest due on long-term loans granted by credit institutions. In the case of the first two exceptions, the requirement for the calculation of interest is that there is a backlog and, in the case of the third exception, such provision is permitted already in the long-term loan agreement. The debtor of a credit institution under a long-term loan agreement is therefore not protected on general terms against overly restrictive interest for late payment and the credit institution itself is privileged in legal transactions in this respect. One of the situations where anatolycism is now legally applicable is the provision of a long-term loan by a credit institution. However, both of these terms are inaccurately explained, which in practice leads to a number of interpretational abuses and consequently to the excessive use of loan interest capitalisations. This project aims to improve the position of consumers in their relations with banks and other credit institutions. Its introduction will counter the increase in the debt of clients of credit institutions and fall into the so-called. the debt loop for the possibility of making an appointment in advance for payment of interest on late interest, so-called. interest paid, without bringing an action. High Izbo! The proposed changes should be assessed positively, they should reduce the practice of lending companies, which often increase the burden on debtors in a way that insults all standards. The implementation of the project will counter the increase in the debt of clients of credit institutions and fall into the so-called. the debt loop for the possibility of making an appointment in advance for payment of interest on late interest, so-called. the compound percentage. However, it should be borne in mind that the repeal of the Article. 482 § 2 will not result in a credit institution not being able to claim interest on outstanding interest, but will be subject to prior action.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u323	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The last 250 years of history of the Polish nation are filled with wars and armed breaks in defence of the independence and sovereignty of the Republic of Poland or with the aim of regaining the freedom and independence of the state. Generations of Poles and all nationalities living in the land of the present and former Republic of Poland laid on the altar the most precious gift - their own life. Those who died in battle, whether in regular formations or in guerrilla troops, in conspiracy, as well as victims of the crimes of invaders and invaders murdered, tortured in prisons, camps and lagras, are all surrounded by honor and respect, and future generations should cultivate their due memory. The independent Republic is obliged to show them equal respect and memory, as are their fallen comrades in arms. For many years, institutions appointed to preserve national identity and care for memorial sites have been involved here, among others. The former Council for the Protection of Remembrance of the Fight and Martyrdom and the Institute of National Remembrance also dealt with these matters by the voivods - there were reports and appeals about taking care of the veterans' graves for natural reasons during the period of peace. The graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland treated as private graves, family graves are not registered, and in their case there is no provision for financial support system. The bill, which today the High Chamber is considering, aims to determine the rules for the care of the graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland from 1768 to 1963. These dates are very important and symbolic. The first date is, of course, the beginning of the first rally in defence of the sovereignty of the Republic of Poland, the bar confederation, the final date is the year of the death of the last soldier of the cursed, steadfast, resistance. Józef Franczak, ps. Dolls. The bill is considered veterans to be participants in armed combat and civil servants of insurgent governments and emigration authorities. These graves are located both in Poland and abroad. The project, which the High Chamber is considering today, assumes: the creation of a collective record of graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland and the introduction of a financial support system for entities taking care of such graves. As far as foundations, associations, churches or religious associations are concerned, support will be in the form of targeted grants. As far as individuals are concerned, support will be in the form of a cash benefit. The institution responsible for creating records of graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland is to be the Institute of National Remembrance. An entry in the register of a grave situated in the territory of the Republic of Poland may be made at the request of a natural person, a foundation, an association, a church or a religious union, as well as of another legal person or organizational unit which is not a legal person or of its own motion. In the case of a grave of a veteran located outside the territory of the Republic of Poland, the entry may be made at the request of a consul of the Republic of Poland or of his own motion. The entry of the grave in the records will be preceded by a survey carried out by the President of the Institute of National Remembrance, or by the grave concerned, meets the criteria of recognition as a grave of a veteran fighting for freedom and independence of Poland. In view of the provision laid down in the draft, according to which the provisions of that law do not apply to war graves within the meaning of the Law of 28 March 1933 on graves and war cemeteries, the entry in the records will be made after confirmation by the competent voivode that the grave concerned has not been entered in the records referred to in the Law on graves and war cemeteries and does not fulfil the conditions for its recognition as a war grave within the meaning of the above. Laws. In addition, it was proposed to require the voivods to inform without delay the president of the Institute about the discovery of a grave which does not meet the conditions to be regarded as a war grave and which in the assessment of the voivodeship can be regarded as a grave of a veteran fighting for freedom and independence of Poland. If the President of the Institute of a particular grave refuses to accept Poland's freedom and independence as the grave of a veteran, the applicant will be entitled to appeal to the College of the Institute of National Remembrance. The project also provides for the publication in the Public Information Bulletin of the Institute of the list of graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland. The grant provided for in the draft law may be granted to finance or co-finance tasks related to the care of the graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland, in particular to cover the expenditure necessary for the construction, repair, transfer and maintenance of these graves. This grant will be granted on the basis of a contract. As regards support for natural persons, such persons will be able to apply for the provision of money by the IPN to cover all or part of the costs of care provided, in particular to cover the expenses necessary for the construction, repair and maintenance of the grave. A benefit agreement will also be concluded in this case. The bill contains, in accordance with the Public Finance Act, a financial rule which provides that the top limit of the budget allocated by the Institute of National Remembrance will be annually - the financial rule applies to the years up to 2028 - an amount of 5275 thousand. zł. The draft proposes that the Act enter into force on 1 January 2019. High Izbo! Mrs. Marshal! Please support the proposed bill. [[Applause]]

This will organize the knowledge of the veterans' graves and allow them to be saved if the family members of the deceased veteran lack personal care. At the same time, the adoption of the proposed law will allow for proper implementation of the constitutional obligation to protect national heritage by preserving and saving the graves of deceased heroes who, due to the passage of time and the absence of heirs, are deprived of the care of loved ones and who are not often cared for by local communities due to lack of knowledge. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u352	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the first reading of the Government's draft law on the graves of veterans fighting for freedom and independence of Poland, print No 2902, paragraph 29, and the first reading of the Commission's draft law on ensuring worthy memory and proper protection of the resting places of the Heroes of the Warsaw Uprising and other persons deserved in the fight for independence and sovereignty of the Polish State, print No 2920, paragraph 30. Our nation of Poland - all citizens of the Republic of Poland... grateful to our ancestors for their work, for the struggle for independence occupied by huge victims, (...) memorials of bitter experiences from the time when fundamental freedoms and human rights were broken in our Homeland - as stated in the preamble to the Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 2 April 1997. From her art. 5 On the other hand, it is clear that the preservation of national heritage is one of the most important tasks of the Republic of Poland and of all public authorities. But not only does the constitution respectfully refer to national heroes. A number of laws, including the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the Consecution of Crimes against the Polish Nationals, declare that it is the duty of the public authorities to preserve the memory of the great number of victims, losses and damages suffered by the Polish people during and after the Second World War and to cultivate the patriotic traditions of the Polish people's struggle with the invaders, Nazism and communism. The preservation of the memory of national heroes is an essential element of every national community, and one of the forms of preservation of this memory is to provide adequate care for the burial places of people who participated in fights for the defence of Poland's independence. It is the responsibility of the State and of all its authorities to ensure that the graves of national heroes are kept in good condition and that they are not wound up, especially in cases where there is no possibility for members of the deceased's family to continue to take care of the resting place. And that is what two today's draft laws in prints 2902 and 2920 refer to. November is a month of special memory of the dead, saints preserved in our memory, about heroes, thanks to which we live today in free and independent Poland. Therefore, from this place, from the heart of Polish freedom and democracy, which is Parliament, I thank all those who contributed to the preparation and submission of these projects in the Sejm. They require clarification and harmonisation, as can be seen from the calculation set out in the explanatory memorandum of the Act, that 7000 zlotys should be sufficient for the renovation or replacement of a single monument, which seems to be far too small, even unreal. It would be necessary to assess this issue after a year of validity of the Act. I also hope to extend the final version of the law, among others. the so-called. memorial sites, of which there are thousands throughout Poland. Often they are forgotten places, which are devastating places that nobody cares about. Funds should also be earmarked for the renovation of such sites. In Warsaw itself there are more than 160 so-called. Thorek's memorial plaques that commemorate the Nazi crime scenes. In addition, we have many other places and plaques that require proper care. Therefore, in further work on the Law, we will make amendments extending the scope of care to be provided by the IPN, including the mentioned memorial sites. It will be a beautiful gift on the occasion of the 100th anniversary of Poland regaining independence for all our national heroes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u361	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the committee's report on the government draft law on the National Debt Register, prints No. 2637 and 2991. The project deals with the definition of rules for maintaining the National Debt Register in the ICT system and serves to implement European regulations. On the basis of the Insolvency Regulation, Member States shall establish and maintain on their territory at least one register, so-called. the bankruptcy register in which information on insolvency proceedings is published immediately after they have been opened. The Regulation sets out a minimum set of publicly available data. The advantages of the project include improving the information of creditors and courts by providing the IT system with timely access to the files of restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings. It is also an asset to prevent parallel insolvency proceedings in the Member States. The establishment of a public electronic access point for information in the e-Justice system will allow access to mandatory information contained in insolvency registers that Member States will make available. The introduction of these changes will also involve increasing the safety of economic turnover, speeding up and improving restructuring and bankruptcy procedures and increasing their efficiency. There are still doubts about the need to share PESEL numbers in the National Debt Register. However, practice shows that this is difficult to avoid. Taking into account the project aspects given, on behalf of the Posel Club I am in favour of adopting this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u363	I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats a position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on the National Debt Register. This project envisages the creation of a National Debt Register. It will be public, so everyone will be able to read the data disclosed in it via the Internet. The project also envisages facilitating the restructuring and bankruptcy of entrepreneurs. This is a response to the serious problems of restructuring and bankruptcy courts resulting from a significant increase in the number of cases, in particular those relating to consumer bankruptcy. Projectors propose to introduce an obligation to submit applications and documents by electronic means in the restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings of entrepreneurs. This will enable the automatic transfer of data from the application submitted by electronic means to the repertoires and the rules of the court and the judge of the Commissioner in question. An electronic service is introduced as a principle in the project, which is intended to speed up the communication of the participants in the proceedings with the court. From this principle, an exception has been proposed to allow applications to be submitted outside the electronic system: employees, maintenance creditors and debtors who are consumers will be able to submit applications in paper form and orally at the transmitting office of each court. The files of restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings are to be kept in electronic form from now on. Access to them will be possible both in the file reading room in court and through a publicly available web portal. The draft also introduces an obligation to submit letters on forms set out in the online system, which will be mandatory for participants and bodies of the proceedings. The principle is also to create a single account for the entity, which will allow for communication with the court in restructuring and bankruptcy cases and registered cases. The proposed law will abolish the obligation to publish notices in the Court and Economic Monitor. This is expected to bring savings of PLN 3.6 million per year to the interested. The newly created National Debt Register will disclose the data of persons subject to restructuring and insolvency proceedings and those subject to ineffective execution. High Izbo! The proposed changes should be assessed positively. They will help to save businesses and reduce the costs of proceedings. Less paper correspondence and a more efficient circulation of documents will allow courts to act more effectively. The advantage of the project is also to facilitate access to court files and significantly reduce the time needed for access to data contained in documents relating to restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings. The European dimension of the changes is also important. The new system will link insolvency registers and the European e-Justice portal, which will act as a central electronic public access point for information in the system. The system will offer a search function in all official languages of the Union institutions to allow access to mandatory information and any other documents and information contained in insolvency registers maintained by Member States, which will also decide to make their resources available via the e-Justice portal.

It is the most important state office to uphold the rights of the child, who is taking steps to ensure that the child is fully and harmoniously developed with respect for his dignity and subjectivity. We do not want to suggest any particular choice for you. However, we want the candidate to be acceptable to everyone. We want to put aside daily political disputes that digest Polish reality and to select a person who will dignifiedly represent an extremely large group of people in our homeland. We should also be able to participate in the selection process which is so important for the Polish official. Therefore, we ask that in order to ensure the welfare of Polish children and families through consensus and regardless of party colours, you choose a candidate or candidate who can act in the interest of the youngest with each party of the political barricade. At the very end we want to share with you a quote from one of the most outstanding Polish educators, called the spokesperson for the rights of the child, Janusz Korczak: there are no children, there are people. Remembering this, we ask that almost 7 million Polish citizens be taken seriously and that their attorney and spokesman be chosen. High Izbo! I hope that we will fulfill this request and this appeal will come to all of us. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u419	Thank you very much, Member. Mr Waldemar Andzel made his statement in writing. Please, Mr Jacek Wilk, a non-attached Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-08-sejm-71-2.u420	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This speech I would like to ask for a very serious discussion, to start a serious discussion about the state of Polish mining and coal mine. As far as this sector of the economy is concerned, once actually crucial for Poland, there are usually two such extreme approaches. First: to subsidize, to supplement at each stage at all times. Two: privatize as soon as possible. A reckless approach to this in both cases leads astray and is, of course, wrong, as demonstrated by a very clear example of the Krupinski mine. The Krupinski Mine, very well-functioning, very well-armed, very technically well-protected and well-connected in terms of transport, was put into bankruptcy and intended for liquidation, although this mine has access to very large, one of the largest in Poland resources, deposits of coal, coke coal, the best type of coal, the deposit 405/1. Although it could function, as it is actually ready to continue mining, it was artificially bankrupted and put up for sale, while the main buyer was to be a company with a share capital of about 100 pounds, which shows that we are dealing with very deep irregularities. It certainly needs to be investigated. But that's not the most important thing. The most important thing is that in order to meet the needs of Polish mining and hard coal mines come out in an amazing way, one can say that saving modern technologies for all of us. These are mainly technologies related to the underground gasification of coal, as well as technologies for the production of synthetic liquid fuels from hard coal. In particular, this first technology gives improbable opportunities and hopes for Polish mining, because it is excellent for processing coal located below the level of 1200 m, i.e. those decks of hard coal, whose extraction under normal circumstances in the current technical state is not profitable. In short, their extraction by traditional methods is hardly an option at the moment. On the other hand, the underground coal gasification technology makes it possible to use these resources in such a way that hydrogen can be produced in huge quantities by this technology. It should be noted that the resources of hard coal located below the level of 1200 m, or the limit of current technical possibilities, are estimated to be very large, even huge, not to say: gigantic, because they are probably 10 times larger than those which are above 1200 m, and therefore, this is an incredible potential which gives Poland the ability to produce billions of cubic meters of hydrogen. Hydrogen can be used primarily in two ways, apart from many other applications. In the first place for electromobility purposes, i.e. the production of hydrogen electric cells, which, given the very rapid development of electromobility in Poland, can be even a salvation for us. Secondly, the same hydrogen can be used in so-called. distributed energy, i.e. in energy clusters, and given that it is considered to be the target model for the development of Polish energy, hydrogen can be simply a fuel, thanks to which Poland will safeguard its energy needs. In short, everything indicates that this approach to mining and coal mines can not only be a salvation for these mines, not only can it give them huge income, but it can also be a cause or factor for the huge development of Silesia, but also for all other areas that these new technologies will use, in particular hydrogen produced by this method. Poland therefore has enormous opportunities to use this technology and this wealth to the extent that it is estimated that this hydrogen can be exported in huge quantities. Therefore, I call on government factors to do so, and I ask that they reflect very strongly on the use of this technology, and first of all on the allocation of funds to build an experimental, demonstration hydrogen production facility using underground coal gasification technology in the Krupinski mine, which is ideally suited for this. Finally, the estimated revenues from this type of production may be so high that thanks to the financial means obtained from them we will even be able to repay Poland's public debts, including the most important debt of the pension system. Thank you so much for listening to the speech.
